| 2026-08-21 | `db/migrations/0065_cert_arm_pinned_sim_start.sql` | **THE MEASUREMENT FIX — harness only, no engine change.** `public.ottoq_cert_arm_start` pins the run's SIM clock to a fixed time of day (`v_sim0 = date_trunc('day', v_now) + 22h`) instead of hardcoding `v_now`. The domain split is the care of the patch: SIM domain moves together (`sim_clock_start/current/end`, both `vehicles.last_state_change` writes, and the dispatch window `dispatched_at`/`scheduled_return_at`); REAL domain stays on `v_now` (`started_at`, `last_tick_at`, `next_tick_due_at` — they schedule the metronome in wall-clock and moving them would strand the run or fire 22 hours of ticks at once). A DO-block assertion fails the migration if the real-clock triple does not survive verbatim. | **Without it no fix in this series can be MEASURED, only verified.** The arm sampled whichever 10-sim-hour slice of `normal_day` began at the wall-clock minute it was started: re-cert #17 (armed 22:40Z) carried 1,075 / 1,098 commands per arm, #18 (armed 00:32Z) carried 1,562 / 1,598 — ~45% more work from the arrival curve alone. Within-round A-vs-B stayed valid throughout (arms are ~2 min apart in one band), so every determinism verdict and every bug in 0045–0064 still stands; but no round could be differenced against another, which is exactly why 0064's throughput effect is logged as unmeasured-because-confounded. 22:00 UTC chosen as a real return-wave slice sitting between the two observed loads. **One trap found and closed before apply:** `ottoq_benchmark_reset` stamps `ottoq_ocpp_chargers.last_heartbeat_at = now()` (REAL), while the proposers gate on `last_heartbeat_at >= <sim clock> - 90 seconds` (`ottoq_honour_reservation_proposal` — the first proposer `ottoq_decide_tick` calls — and `ottoq_l2_propose_stall_assignment`; `ottoq_book_appointment` / `ottoq_plan_opportunistic_charges` / `ottoq_sim_prearrival_contracts` use a 35-minute window). Those lined up only because the sim clock equalled the arming minute. Pinned, a real-clock heartbeat reads OFFLINE, every charger silently drops out of every proposal on tick 1, and nothing raises. The migration therefore also stamps the depot's chargers to `v_sim0`, the same domain `ottoq_sim_advance_tick_world` re-stamps them in on every subsequent tick. `ottoq_eval_hw_002_charger_state` needed no change — its `v_now` is `COALESCE(p_context->>'now_ts', NOW())` and the decide path wires the sim clock in. | Applied 2026-08-22 00:48Z (founder-merged PR #65) via `apply_migration`; post-md5 `00314bb96f99e5beaa6fb2b4cd8b9143`, which **matches the locally computed post-image byte-for-byte**. **PROVEN IN THE FIRST PINNED RUN (re-cert #19):** `sim_clock_start = 2026-08-22 22:00:00+00` exactly, on both arms, with `tick_count = 0` at shield time. **The heartbeat trap was real and is closed — measured, not argued:** after tick 1 of arm A, 40 chargers were visible to the proposer window (`last_heartbeat_at >= sim_clock_current - 90s`) and `noop_no_candidate` was **0**. On apply, the first pinned-clock pair becomes the BASELINE every later throughput claim is differenced against. |

Tick 9 agreed byte-for-byte (`reservation_honoured`), so the divergence is introduced precisely here; the outer vehicle cursor is already total (`current_soc ASC, v.id`), and one tie suffices because the first vehicle consumes a stall via `v_used` and the second is handed the other. Defect (2) is the same class as 0065's charger-heartbeat trap: masked while the sim clock equalled the arming minute, exposed once 0065 pinned it ~22h away; its error direction is conservative (suppresses capacity, never steals a stall), which is why #19 still ran healthy. | Applied 2026-08-22 01:21Z (founder-merged PR #68) via the `execute_sql` fallback; post-md5 `3d7fa12fec5fe860854d8416dd8e4840`, **matching the dry-run exactly**; sim-domain move and tiebreak both present. **RESULT — re-cert #20 (arms `677bca9c` / `43fbecf4`) PASSES: `ticks_identical = 20/20`, `deterministic = TRUE`.** The prediction held precisely: the tick-10 stall swap is gone and every downstream divergence with it. That was wrong *and accidentally protective* — it never handed away a hold a vehicle was still travelling toward. The corrected test offers stalls whose sim reservation expired, which are exactly the contended ones; the proposer's filter passes at propose time but the confirm walk runs a tick (30 sim-min) later and the stall is gone. Same class as 0064, now on the proposal path. **The fix is NOT to restore `now()`** — that comparison is wrong regardless — but to decline a stall still reserved to a DIFFERENT vehicle irrespective of expiry (`reserved_by IS NULL OR reserved_by = v_veh.id`), restoring the protection deliberately. | In-place mechanism; pre-image pin `c99394c4cbd7436574e7a17b02dbc348`; both anchors pre-verified read-only (exactly one occurrence each). fact about work whose reasoning already lives in the migration files, this block is
generated from those files by `scripts/gen-migration-index.py`. It answers *what is in the
engine and where to read about it*; it does not pretend to answer *what was verified*, which
is what the narrative column above is for. Each file's own header and APPLIED footer carry
that.

`applied` is `yes` when the file itself records having been applied, `—` when it says nothing
either way. A version of `APPLIED-NO-LEDGER-ROW` or `UNVERIFIED-NO-LEDGER-ROW` means there is
no `supabase_migrations` row for the file — see task G18 and Section E of
`scripts/check-drift.sql`.
